- path: src\composables\usePanoramaSnap.ts
  content: |
      import { onMounted, onUnmounted, ref, watch, type Ref } from 'vue'
      
      export function usePanoramaSnap(container: Ref<HTMLElement | null>) {
          const activeIndex = ref(0)
          const panels = ref<HTMLElement[]>([])
      
          let observer: IntersectionObserver | null = null
          let lastEmitted = -1
      
          const collectPanels = () => {
              const el = container.value
              if (!el) {
                  panels.value = []
                  return
              }
              panels.value = Array.from(el.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('[data-panorama-panel]'))
          }
      
          const emitActive = () => {
              const index = activeIndex.value
              if (index === lastEmitted) return
              lastEmitted = index
          }
      
          const scrollTo = (el: HTMLElement) => {
              el.scrollIntoView({ inline: 'start', block: 'nearest', behavior: 'smooth' })
          }
      
          const scrollToIndex = (index: number) => {
              const target = panels.value[index]
              if (target) scrollTo(target)
          }
      
          const setupObserver = () => {
              collectPanels()
              if (observer) {
                  observer.disconnect()
                  observer = null
              }
              const el = container.value
              if (!el || panels.value.length === 0) return
      
              const root = el.parentElement ?? undefined
              observer = new IntersectionObserver(
                  (entries) => {
                      let best: IntersectionObserverEntry | null = null
                      for (const entry of entries) {
                          if (!best || entry.intersectionRatio > best.intersectionRatio) best = entry
                      }
                      if (!best) return
                      const idx = panels.value.indexOf(best.target as HTMLElement)
                      if (idx >= 0 && idx !== activeIndex.value) {
                          activeIndex.value = idx
                          emitActive()
                      }
                  },
                  { root, threshold: [0, 0.5, 1] },
              )
              panels.value.forEach((panel) => observer!.observe(panel))
          }
      
          watch(container, () => setupObserver(), { flush: 'post' })
      
          onMounted(() => {
              setupObserver()
              window.addEventListener('resize', collectPanels)
          })
      
          onUnmounted(() => {
              observer?.disconnect()
              window.removeEventListener('resize', collectPanels)
          })
      
          return { activeIndex, panels, scrollTo, scrollToIndex, refresh: collectPanels }
      }
      
- path: src\components\panorama\PanoramaView.vue
  content: |
      <script setup lang="ts">
      import { computed, ref, onMounted, onUnmounted } from 'vue'
      import type { Experience, Project, Skill } from '@/types'
      import PanoramaIntro from './PanoramaIntro.vue'
      import PanoramaProjects from './PanoramaProjects.vue'
      import PanoramaExperience from './PanoramaExperience.vue'
      import PanoramaContact from './PanoramaContact.vue'
      import PanoramaProjectDetail from './PanoramaProjectDetail.vue'
      
      const props = defineProps<{
          skills: Skill[]
          experience: Experience[]
          projects: Project[]
          copyBtnText: string
      }>()
      
      const emit = defineEmits<{
          copy: []
          sectionChange: [section: 'about' | 'projects' | 'experience' | 'contact']
      }>()
      
      const viewport = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)
      const selectedProject = ref<Project | null>(null)
      const projectsWidth = ref(0)
      const lastSection = ref<'about' | 'projects' | 'experience' | 'contact'>('about')
      
      const sections = computed(() => [
          { id: 'about', title: 'about', vertical: true, component: 'intro' as const },
          { id: 'projects', title: 'projects', vertical: false, component: 'projects' as const },
          { id: 'experience', title: 'experience', vertical: true, component: 'experience' as const },
          { id: 'contact', title: 'contact', vertical: false, component: 'contact' as const },
      ])
      
      const updateActiveSection = () => {
          const el = viewport.value
          if (!el) return
          const rect = el.getBoundingClientRect()
          const mid = rect.left + rect.width / 2
          let active: typeof lastSection.value = 'about'
          for (const sec of sections.value) {
              const node = el.querySelector<HTMLElement>(`#${sec.id}`)
              if (!node) continue
              const r = node.getBoundingClientRect()
              if (r.left <= mid && r.right >= mid) {
                  active = sec.component === 'intro' ? 'about'
                      : sec.component === 'projects' ? 'projects'
                      : sec.component === 'experience' ? 'experience'
                      : 'contact'
                  break
              }
          }
          if (active !== lastSection.value) {
              lastSection.value = active
              emit('sectionChange', active)
          }
      }
      
      let scrollTimer: ReturnType<typeof requestAnimationFrame> | null = null
      const onScroll = () => {
          if (scrollTimer) return
          scrollTimer = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
              scrollTimer = null
              updateActiveSection()
          })
      }
      
      const navigateToSection = (section: 'about' | 'projects' | 'experience' | 'contact') => {
          const track = viewport.value
          if (!track) return
      
          // For experience, scroll to the projects snap-anchor to show both projects (right half) and experience
          const targetId = section === 'experience' ? 'projects-experience-view' : section
          const node = track.querySelector<HTMLElement>(`#${targetId}`)
          if (!node) return
      
          const gutter = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(track).scrollPaddingLeft) || 0
          const target =
              node.getBoundingClientRect().left -
              track.getBoundingClientRect().left +
              track.scrollLeft -
              gutter
      
          track.scrollTo({ left: Math.max(0, target), behavior: 'smooth' })
      }
      
      const openProject = (project: Project) => {
          selectedProject.value = project
      }
      
      const closeProject = () => {
          selectedProject.value = null
      }
      
      const sectionWidth = (id: string) =>
          id === 'projects' && projectsWidth.value > 0 ? `${projectsWidth.value}px` : undefined
      
      defineExpose({ scrollToSection: navigateToSection })
      
      onMounted(() => {
          if (viewport.value) {
              viewport.value.addEventListener('scroll', onScroll, { passive: true })
              updateActiveSection()
          }
      })
      
      onUnmounted(() => {
          if (viewport.value) viewport.value.removeEventListener('scroll', onScroll)
          if (scrollTimer) cancelAnimationFrame(scrollTimer)
      })
      </script>
      
      <template>
          <div
              id="app"
              class="h-screen w-screen flex flex-col bg-white text-black overflow-hidden select-none dark:bg-zinc-950 dark:text-white"
          >
              <main
                  ref="viewport"
                  class="panorama-track flex-1 min-h-0 w-full overflow-x-auto overflow-y-hidden relative"
                  tabindex="0"
              >
                  <div class="track-inner flex items-stretch h-full pt-6 pb-8 pl-6 sm:pl-32">
                      <section
                          v-for="section in sections"
                          :id="section.id"
                          :key="section.id"
                          class="snap-section flex-none flex flex-col"
                          :class="section.id === 'projects' ? 'snap-wide' : ''"
                          :style="{ width: sectionWidth(section.id) }"
                      >
                          <div class="mb-4 flex items-baseline justify-between">
                              <h2 class="text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-normal lowercase tracking-tight">
                                  {{ section.title }}
                              </h2>
                          </div>
      
                          <div
                              class="flex-1 min-h-0"
                              :class="section.vertical && 'overflow-y-auto overscroll-y-contain'"
                          >
                              <PanoramaIntro
                                  v-if="section.component === 'intro'"
                                  :skills="props.skills"
                                  @navigate="navigateToSection"
                              />
                              <PanoramaProjects
                                  v-else-if="section.component === 'projects'"
                                  :projects="props.projects"
                                  @select="openProject"
                                  @width-change="projectsWidth = $event"
                              />
                              <PanoramaExperience
                                  v-else-if="section.component === 'experience'"
                                  :experience="props.experience"
                              />
                              <PanoramaContact
                                  v-else-if="section.component === 'contact'"
                                  :copy-btn-text="props.copyBtnText"
                                  @copy="emit('copy')"
                              />
                          </div>
      
                          <div
                              v-if="section.id === 'projects'"
                              id="projects-experience-view"
                              class="snap-anchor"
                              style="left: 50%"
                              aria-hidden="true"
                          />
                      </section>
                  </div>
              </main>
      
              <Transition name="panorama-swap" mode="out-in">
                  <PanoramaProjectDetail
                      v-if="selectedProject"
                      key="detail"
                      :project="selectedProject"
                      @back="closeProject"
                  />
              </Transition>
          </div>
      </template>
      
      <style scoped>

          scroll-snap-type: x mandatory;
          scroll-padding-left: 1.5rem;
          scroll-padding-right: 1.5rem;

      }

      }

          }

          }
      }
      
      .panorama-swap-enter-active,

      <script setup lang="ts">
      import { computed, onMounted, onUnmounted, ref } from 'vue'
      import type { Project } from '@/types'
      import { usePanoramaSnap } from '@/composables/usePanoramaSnap'
      
      const props = defineProps<{
          project: Project
      }>()
      
      const emit = defineEmits<{
          back: []
      }>()
      
      const container = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)
      
      usePanoramaSnap(container)
      
      const descriptionLines = computed(() =>
          props.project.description
              .split('\n')
              .map((line) => line.trim())
              .filter(Boolean),
      )
      
      const shouldSplitDescription = computed(
          () => props.project.description.length > 520 && descriptionLines.value.length > 4,
      )
      
      const splitIndex = computed(() => Math.max(1, Math.ceil(descriptionLines.value.length / 2)))
      
      const firstDescriptionHtml = computed(() =>
          shouldSplitDescription.value
              ? props.project.descriptionHtml.split('\n').slice(0, splitIndex.value).join('\n')
              : props.project.descriptionHtml,
      )
      
      const secondDescriptionHtml = computed(() =>
          props.project.descriptionHtml.split('\n').slice(splitIndex.value).join('\n'),
      )
      
      const heroImage = computed(() => props.project.images[0] ?? props.project.thumbnailImages[0])
      
      const handleKeydown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
          if (event.key === 'Escape') {
              event.preventDefault()
              emit('back')
          }
      }
      
      onMounted(() => {
          document.addEventListener('keydown', handleKeydown)
      })
      
      onUnmounted(() => {
          document.removeEventListener('keydown', handleKeydown)
      })
      </script>

```

Yes for the main view — and *only* CSS there. But not for the project detail view.

**`PanoramaView.vue`** — native browser snapping:
- `.panorama-track` has `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` + `scroll-padding-left/right` (with `overscroll-behavior-x: contain`, `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch`).
- Each `section.snap-section` (and the invisible `#projects-experience-view` anchor) has `scroll-snap-align: start` + `scroll-snap-stop: always`.

There is no JS wheel/drag/touch emulation in that file. JavaScript only:
- runs `scrollTo({ behavior: 'smooth' })` for nav clicks (`navigateToSection`),
- tracks the active section with a `scroll` + `requestAnimationFrame` listener,
- exposes `scrollToSection` to the parent.

**`PanoramaProjectDetail.vue`** — no snapping at all. Its `.panorama-scroll` / `.panorama-panel` rules in `src/assets/main.css` are just `overflow-x: auto` flex panels with fixed widths; there's no `scroll-snap-type` or `scroll-snap-align` anywhere for that view. It calls `usePanoramaSnap`, but that composable only does an `IntersectionObserver` for `activeIndex` plus a `scrollIntoView({ inline: 'start', behavior: 'smooth' })` helper — and its return values are discarded at the call site, so nothing snaps on wheel/drag/keyboard there.

So: the panorama is CSS-snap-driven, while the project-detail scroller drifts freely (and it still carries the now-unused `usePanoramaScroll.ts` / `usePanoramaSnap` scaffolding that `docs/refactor-plan.md` flags). If you want the detail view to snap too, it's a CSS-only addition — `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` on `.panorama-scroll` and `scroll-snap-align: start` on `.panorama-panel` (plus `scroll-snap-stop: always` if you want one panel per gesture).

- path: src\components\panorama\PanoramaProjects.vue
  content: |
      <script setup lang="ts">
      import { computed, onMounted, onUnmounted, ref, watch } from 'vue'
      import type { Project } from '@/types'
      import PanoramaProjectTile from './PanoramaProjectTile.vue'
      
      const props = defineProps<{
          projects: Project[]
      }>()
      
      const emit = defineEmits<{
          select: [project: Project]
          widthChange: [width: number]
      }>()
      
      const GAP = 16
      const COLUMNS = 5
      const MOBILE_COLUMNS = 2
      const PREVIOUS_COLUMNS = 6
      const MAX_TILE_WIDTH = 400
      const VIEWPORT_GUTTER = 64
      const MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 640
      const MOBILE_WIDTH_RATIO = 0.85
      
      const container = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)
      const containerHeight = ref(0)
      const viewportWidth = ref(window.innerWidth)
      
      let resizeObserver: ResizeObserver | null = null
      
      const updateViewportWidth = () => {
          viewportWidth.value = window.innerWidth
      }
      
      onMounted(() => {
          updateViewportWidth()
          window.addEventListener('resize', updateViewportWidth)
      
          resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver((entries) => {
              const entry = entries[0]
              if (entry) containerHeight.value = entry.contentRect.height
          })
      
          if (container.value) resizeObserver.observe(container.value)
      })
      
      onUnmounted(() => {
          resizeObserver?.disconnect()
          window.removeEventListener('resize', updateViewportWidth)
      })
      
      const orderedProjects = computed(() => {
          const items = props.projects
          if (items.length <= COLUMNS) return items
      
          const previousRows = Math.ceil(items.length / PREVIOUS_COLUMNS)
          const removedColumn = PREVIOUS_COLUMNS - 1
          const kept: Project[] = []
          const removed: Project[] = []
      
          for (let row = 0; row < previousRows; row++) {
              for (let column = 0; column < PREVIOUS_COLUMNS; column++) {
                  const project = items[row * PREVIOUS_COLUMNS + column]
                  if (!project) continue
      
                  if (column === removedColumn) removed.push(project)
                  else kept.push(project)
              }
          }
      
          return [...kept, ...removed]
      })
      
      const isMobile = computed(() => viewportWidth.value < MOBILE_BREAKPOINT)
      
      const columns = computed(() => (isMobile.value ? MOBILE_COLUMNS : COLUMNS))
      
      const gridProjects = computed(() => (isMobile.value ? props.projects : orderedProjects.value))
      
      const rows = computed(() => Math.max(1, Math.ceil(props.projects.length / columns.value)))
      
      const tileWidth = computed(() => {
          if (containerHeight.value <= 0) return 0
      
          const availableWidth = isMobile.value
              ? viewportWidth.value * MOBILE_WIDTH_RATIO
              : viewportWidth.value - VIEWPORT_GUTTER
          const maxByWidth = (availableWidth - (columns.value - 1) * GAP) / columns.value
      
          if (isMobile.value) return Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_TILE_WIDTH, maxByWidth))
      
          const maxByHeight = (containerHeight.value - (rows.value - 1) * GAP) / (rows.value * 0.75)
      
          return Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_TILE_WIDTH, maxByHeight, maxByWidth))
      })
      
      const gridStyle = computed(() => {
          const size = tileWidth.value
          return {
              gridTemplateColumns: `repeat(${columns.value}, ${size}px)`,
              gridTemplateRows: `repeat(${rows.value}, ${size * 0.75}px)`,
              gap: `${GAP}px`,
              justifyContent: 'start',
          }
      })
      
      const gridWidth = computed(() => tileWidth.value * columns.value + (columns.value - 1) * GAP)
      
      watch(
          gridWidth,
          (width) => {
              if (tileWidth.value > 0) emit('widthChange', width)
          },
          { immediate: true },
      )
      </script>
      
      <template>
          <div
              ref="container"
              class="h-full pb-10 pt-0 sm:pt-6"
              :class="isMobile && 'overflow-y-auto overscroll-y-contain'"
              :data-panorama-vertical="isMobile ? '' : undefined"
          >
              <div class="grid" :style="gridStyle">
                  <PanoramaProjectTile
                      v-for="project in gridProjects"
                      :key="project.id"
                      :project="project"
                      @select="emit('select', project)"
                  />
              </div>
          </div>
      </template>
      
- path: src\components\panorama\PanoramaProjectDetail.vue
  content: |
      <script setup lang="ts">import { computed, onMounted, onUnmounted } from 'vue'
      import type { Project } from '@/types'
      
      const props = defineProps<{
          project: Project
      }>()
      
      const emit = defineEmits<{
          back: []
      }>()
      
      const descriptionLines = computed(() =>
          props.project.description
              .split('\n')
              .map((line) => line.trim())
              .filter(Boolean),
      )
      
      const shouldSplitDescription = computed(
          () => props.project.description.length > 520 && descriptionLines.value.length > 4,
      )
      
      const splitIndex = computed(() => Math.max(1, Math.ceil(descriptionLines.value.length / 2)))
      
      const firstDescriptionHtml = computed(() =>
          shouldSplitDescription.value
              ? props.project.descriptionHtml.split('\n').slice(0, splitIndex.value).join('\n')
              : props.project.descriptionHtml,
      )
      
      const secondDescriptionHtml = computed(() =>
          props.project.descriptionHtml.split('\n').slice(splitIndex.value).join('\n'),
      )
      
      const heroImage = computed(() => props.project.images[0] ?? props.project.thumbnailImages[0])
      
      const handleKeydown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
          if (event.key === 'Escape') {
              event.preventDefault()
              emit('back')
          }
      }
      
      onMounted(() => {
          document.addEventListener('keydown', handleKeydown)
      })
      
      onUnmounted(() => {
          document.removeEventListener('keydown', handleKeydown)
      })
      </script>
      
      <template>

> 2. Snap positions derived from standard metrics, not DOM measurement: index-based. For each section with span S, snap offset = cumulative(previous spans × (unit + gap)) adjusted so wide sections can snap to any of their S internal offsets (e.g. wide projects section snaps at start of each unit within it) — that's the "automatically knows which parts of the wide section to scroll to snap" requirement.

So instead of native CSS scroll-snap (which snaps at declared align points and can jump multiple), the framework computes a snap-point list = for each section, for each unit offset within its span, one stop. Active stop tracked by cumulative travel. But they explicitly ask CSS-only? No — they ask framework to know widths. Native CSS snap can do this if we place invisible snap-anchor elements per unit inside wide sections — but they said wide section is continuous, not compartmentalized into containers with gaps. This is robust, replicates one-unit-per-swipe regardless of section spans, and wide sections simply occupy `span` indices. Also gives free `activeIndex` and progress.

> Let me think carefully about what best meets requirements:
> 
> Requirements restated:
> - Replicable, project-agnostic (a `<Panorama>` component + `<PanoramaSection :span="n">` slot-based wrapper; no data-specific props).
> - Wide section consumes more than one section width, measured in units of regular section width (integer spans).
> - Framework automatically knows snap positions within wide section → snaps at each unit boundary inside it.
> - Regular sections adapt to content height (no fixed content measurement driving width).
> - Wide section is a continuous container (no internal gap-separated panels).
> - Framework's only concern: travel distance; each tick/scroll/swipe = one regular section width.
> 
> Design:
> 
> Constants via CSS custom properties on the track root:
> ```
> --pano-unit: 50vw (desktop) / 85vw (mobile)
> --pano-gap: 4rem / 1rem
> --pano-stride: calc(var(--pano-unit) + var(--pano-gap))
> --pano-pad: 8rem / 1.5rem  (scroll-padding)
> ```
> Section width formula for span n:
> ```
> width: calc(var(--pano-unit) * n + var(--pano-gap) * (n - 1))
> ```
> so a 2-unit section is exactly two units + the internal gap it swallows — that's what keeps the outer rhythm constant. That is the key formula. Currently snap-wide = 100vw while unit=50vw and gap 4rem → misaligned; fixing this formula is the core CSS change.
> 
> Snap grid: define stops in JS from section metadata (spans array, read from DOM `data-span` once, or from slot props). Stops absolute x = scrollPadLeft + i*stride where i counts units. A wide section with span 2 owns stops i and i+1; snapping to either is a valid stop inside continuous content — no need to split DOM.
> 
> Scroll engine options:
> 
> Option A (CSS-only with anchors): render inside each wide section a `.snap-grid` overlay with `span` invisible anchors at left offsets 0, stride, 2*stride... Actually anchors inside the wide section positioned at `left: calc(var(--pano-stride) * k)`; `scroll-snap-align: start`. CSS handles gesture physics, one-per-swipe via `scroll-snap-stop: always`. Framework reads active index via scroll position (rAF, cheap) or IntersectionObserver on anchors. Continuous wide content preserved (anchors are 1px, absolutely positioned, no layout/gap).
> 
> Option B (JS-driven snap): wheel/touch/key handlers compute direction, `index += ±1`, `scrollTo(index * stride)`, native smooth behavior, disable CSS snap. Deterministic, no anchor elements; works identically on any content; gives progress. Risk: re-implementing physics (momentum, trackpads fire many wheel events → need debounce/lock), accessibility of scroll container, and touch needs non-passive listeners. That's exactly the machinery refactor-plan wanted to delete.
> 
> Recommendation: A as the mechanism (native, accessible, momentum preserved), with the JS layer only for index math + programmatic navigation (`scrollToIndex`), which is what PanoramaView already does. The "anchor per unit" generation is generic and content-agnostic. And it fixes the current anchor hack (`#projects-experience-view` hardcoded at left:50%) — becomes just stop index.
> 
> Now active index: currently PanoramaView uses scroll position + section mid; better: compute `index = round((scrollLeft - padLeft) / stride)` directly — pure travel math, no DOM per-element queries. Then section = the section whose [startIndex, startIndex+span) contains index. This matches "framework only concern is how much it traveled".
> 
> API design (project-agnostic):
> 
> ```vue
> <Panorama :spans="[1,2,1,1]" :labels="['about','projects','experience','contact']" @change="...">
>   <template #default="{ snapTo }"> ... content ... </template>
> </Panorama>
> <!-- or -->
> <PanoramaSection :span="1" title="about">...</PanoramaSection>
> ```
> Better: `<Panorama>` scans child `<PanoramaSection :span>` via provide/inject to build the stop table without prop duplication. Slots-based with explicit spans array is simpler and more predictable; provide/inject lets sections declare span locally and the parent computes cumulative offsets reactively. I'd propose provide/inject registry: each PanoramaSection registers {id, span, el}, watcher recomputes stop map. But for a Vue SFC generic component, simple props array + a `<PanoramaSection>` that injects is nice. Plan should propose component sketch.
> 
> Also need: mobile behavior (unit = 85vw, wide sections span n units of 85vw) — the current CSS overrides .snap-wide to 85vw on mobile, meaning wide sections lose their wideness on mobile. Decide: keep wide spans on mobile (2×85vw) or force 1? Plan: keep spans; wide content is horizontally laid out? For projects grid on mobile it stacks vertically, so span should be configurable per breakpoint (`:span="{ default: 2, mobile: 1 }"`). Mention.

> Also content overflow: with fixed widths, wide sections must handle their own internal scrolling (vertical) — the framework stays out of it.

> 
> Also keyboard: tabindex=0 + ArrowLeft/Right handling → move ±1 stop (native scroll containers don't snap on arrow keys reliably; currently no key handler in PanoramaView). Mention as part of framework.
> 
> Also ResizeObserver not needed since widths are CSS-derived; but unit changes at breakpoint → recompute stops if cached in px; better derive in JS from getComputedStyle of one probe element or just use CSS custom props and let CSS do snapping, keeping JS purely index math from scrollLeft/stride read once per resize.

> Write it well-structured with code sketches for the width formula and stop table.

Read the current panorama files and the CSS metrics. Here's the plan.

## 1. What's project-specific today

The layout model is fine; the *deduction* of widths is ad-hoc and coupled to content:

| Coupling | Where | Why it breaks generality |
|---|---|---|
| `snap-wide` = `100vw` while units are `50vw` | `PanoramaView.vue` scoped CSS | A 2-unit section is 100vw but 2 units + 1 gap is `100vw + 4rem`, so stops drift out of the grid |
| `widthChange` → `sectionWidth()` | `PanoramaProjects.vue` → `PanoramaView.vue` | Content measures itself and tells the framework how wide the section is — the framework becomes content-dependent |
| `#projects-experience-view` at `left: 50%` | `PanoramaView.vue` | A named DOM hack standing in for "second stop of a 2-span section" |
| `.snap-section` widths re-declared per breakpoint in CSS, `85vw` kills wideness on mobile | `PanoramaView.vue` CSS | Wideness is a decorative class, not a declared quantity |
| Track padding (`pl-6 sm:pl-32`) vs `scroll-padding-left` duplicated | `PanoramaView.vue` template + CSS | Two sources of truth for the snap origin |

Everything else (sections, headers, slot content) already generalizes.

## 2. Target model: one unit, one stride, an integer span per section

The framework knows exactly two numbers, both CSS-derived, one breakpoint each:

```
--pano-unit   50vw (desktop) | 85vw (≤639px)     /* one regular section *width*  */
--pano-gap    4rem          | 1rem
--pano-pad    8rem          | 1.5rem              /* scroll-padding, also track padding */
--pano-stride calc(--pano-unit + --pano-gap)      /* travel per tick */
```

A section's width is a pure function of its span `n`:

```css
.pano-section {
  flex: none;
  width: calc(var(--pano-unit) * var(--n) + var(--pano-gap) * (var(--n) - 1));
}
```

This is the whole trick: a 2-span section is **two units plus the gap it swallows**, so the outer rhythm is identical whether a section is 1 or 3 units wide. Today's `100vw` is exactly the bug that makes the wide section look "compartmentalized" — fix the formula and it's one continuous box with no internal gaps.

Note the mobile override disappears: a 2-span section on mobile is `2 × 85vw`. If a specific content type should collapse (the project grid reflows to a single column), that's declared, not inferred: `:span="{ base: 1, sm: 2 }"`.

## 3. Stop grid = travel × unit index

Because widths are now standard, the stop table is computable without reading the DOM:

```
stop i (0-based)  →  x = pad + i * stride
rank(section)     →  [ startIndex, startIndex + span )
section span n    →  owns n consecutive stops
```

- A 1-span section owns 1 stop. A 2-span section owns 2 stops, `startIndex` and `startIndex + 1`, both *inside one continuous container*. That's "the framework automatically knows which parts of the wide section to scroll to snap" — no sub-containers, no per-child measurement.
- Active state is travel math, not DOM probing:
  `index = round((scrollLeft - pad) / stride)`; `activeSection = the section whose [start, start+span) contains index`. This replaces `updateActiveSection`'s `getBoundingClientRect` loop and the mid-point scan.
- `scrollToSection(id)` = `scrollTo(startIndex * stride + pad)`.
- The "experience" peek becomes generic: `scrollToSection(id, { lead: 1 })` → target `startIndex - 1` (clamped), i.e. land on the last stop of the preceding section. No `#projects-experience-view`, no `left: 50%`.

## 4. Mechanism: CSS does the snapping, JS only does indices and targets

Keep native `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` + `scroll-snap-stop: always` (one section per swipe, momentum, touch physics, accessibility all free). The unit stops just need to exist as snap targets. Two ways to materialize them:

**Recommended — overlay anchor grid per section.** Inside each section, render `span` absolutely-positioned 1px anchors at `left: calc(var(--pano-stride) * k)`, `top:0; bottom:0; pointer-events:none; scroll-snap-align:start`. They're invisible, take no layout space, and a 2-span section stays visually continuous. The anchor count comes from `span`, not from content — fully agnostic.

**Rejected — JS wheel/touch interception.** Deterministic `index += ±1; scrollTo(index*stride)` sounds like "only concern is travel", but you'd re-implement trackpad event storms, momentum, touch non-passive listeners and keyboard focus behavior — the 456-line `usePanoramaScroll.ts` we just deleted. Native snap already gives exactly one-stride travel per gesture when `scroll-snap-stop: always` and stops are stride-spaced. Keep JS at: read index, smooth-scroll, handle ArrowLeft/Right.

## 5. Component API (drop into any project)

```
<Panorama v-model:index="i" @section-change="...">
  <PanoramaSection id="about"      title="about">      ...any content... </PanoramaSection>
  <PanoramaSection id="projects"   title="projects" :span="2"> ...any content... </PanoramaSection>
  <PanoramaSection id="experience" title="experience"> ...any content... </PanoramaSection>
</Panorama>
```

- `Panorama` owns: metrics (`--pano-unit/gap/pad`), track, snap anchors, index math, `scrollToSection(id, {lead})`, `defineExpose` for the header nav, `section-change` emission (derived from index).
- `PanoramaSection` owns: its `span` (registers `{ id, span, order }` upward via provide/inject so the parent builds the stop table without a parallel props array), its width from `calc`, optional internal vertical overflow, its title.
- Content never reports width upward. `PanoramaProjects` loses `widthChange`, `containerHeight`-based width feedback and the `VIEWPORT_GUTTER`/viewport arithmetic; its grid sizes from **its own box** (container query or `ResizeObserver` on itself) to fill the declared span. Content adapts *inside* a fixed width; the framework never adapts to content.
- Vertical scrolling stays the section's business (that's why `data-panorama-vertical` exists) — the framework shouldn't know.

## 6. Implementation steps

1. **Extract metrics into CSS custom properties** on the track root, remove per-breakpoint `.snap-section` width overrides, unify track padding with `scroll-padding` (single `--pano-pad`). *Accept: no `50vw`/`100vw`/`85vw` literals outside the token block.*
2. **Add `Panorama.vue` + `PanoramaSection.vue`** as generic components (new dir `src/components/panorama/framework/`), with the span→width formula and the anchor-grid renderer. *Accept: a 3-section demo with spans 1,2,1 snaps at 0, 1×stride, 2×stride, 3×stride.*
3. **Port `PanoramaView.vue`** to compose the two generic components; delete `sections` array's `component`/`vertical` metadata in favor of slot content; derive `sectionChange` from index; replace `#projects-experience-view` with `scrollToSection('experience', { lead: 1 })`. *Accept: header nav, active highlight, and the projects/experience peek behave as today at 375/768/1440px.*
4. **Strip width feedback from `PanoramaProjects.vue`** (remove `widthChange` emit, viewport-width math, `VIEWPORT_GUTTER`), size the grid from the section box; assert a 2-span section hugs exactly two units. *Accept: adding a 4th column of tiles doesn't change section width — it reflows tiles.*
5. **Port `PanoramaProjectDetail.vue`** onto the framework (each panel = `<PanoramaSection :span="1">`), which finally gives the detail view snapping. *Accept: one panel per swipe; Esc/Back unchanged.*
6. **Keyboard/ARIA pass**: ArrowLeft/Right → ±1 stop, `aria-roledescription="carousel"`, `aria-live` off for the active heading. *Accept: keyboard-only traversal of every stop, including both stops of the wide section.*
7. **Document + delete**: rewrite `docs/refactor-plan.md` into a short framework README (tokens, span semantics, API), drop the stale composable references.

## 7. Edge cases to decide up front

- **Fractional spans**: forbid. `span` is a positive integer; `span: 1.5` has no valid stop grid.
- **Last section end padding**: the final stop should show trailing `--pano-pad`; needs one trailing spacer sized `--pano-pad` (generic, not per-view).
- **Sections shorter than the tallest content**: width is fixed, so content must scroll internally or use `min-h`; framework ignores it (intentional).
- **Resize across the 640px breakpoint**: anchors are percentages of the *stride*, so they re-align automatically; only the JS index needs recomputation from `scrollLeft`, which happens on the existing `scroll`/resize listener — no ResizeObserver on content.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion`**: swap `behavior: 'smooth'` → `'auto'`.

## 8. Definition of done

`Panorama.vue` + `PanoramaSection.vue` contain no reference to projects, skills, experience or contact; spans are the only width input; every section is a continuous box; strides are uniform at each breakpoint (verified by `scrollLeft` at each stop being an exact multiple of `stride`); both the main view and the project detail view use the same components.

The riskiest step is 3 — `PanoramaView`'s header/nav behavior and the experience peek are the two places where today's layout hacks encode real UX intent, so port those first and check them before deleting the old markup.

**🛠 Write File**

````json
{
  "path": "docs/panorama-framework.md",
  "instructions": "Document the panorama framework contract: metrics, span formula, stop table, API, rules.",
  "content": "# Panorama framework\n\nA horizontal, snap-per-section scroller with no knowledge of the content it holds. Sections\ndeclare **how many units wide** they are; the framework derives snap stops from that number\nalone.\n\n## Metrics\n\nTwo numbers per breakpoint, exposed as CSS custom properties on the scroll container:\n\n| Token         | Desktop (`>= 640px`) | Mobile (`< 640px`) | Meaning                                  |\n| ------------- | -------------------- | ------------------ | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `--pano-unit` | `50vw`               | `85vw`             | width of one regular (span 1) section    |\n| `--pano-gap`  | `4rem`               | `1rem`             | space between sections                   |\n| `--pano-pad`  | `8rem`               | `1.5rem`           | `scroll-padding` / leading track padding |\n| `--pano-stride` | `unit + gap`       | `unit + gap`       | travel per tick, one regular section     |\n\nA section of span `n` is sized by formula, never by its content:\n\n```css\nwidth: calc(var(--pano-unit) * n + var(--pano-gap) * (n - 1));\n```\n\nThe `(n - 1)` term swallows the internal gaps, so a 2-span section is exactly two units plus the\ngap it covers. Sections stay on one uniform rhythm regardless of span, which is what makes the\nstops computable up front. A wide section is **one continuous box** - nothing is nested or\nspaced inside it by the framework.\n\n## Stops\n\n```\nstop i  ->  x = pad + i * stride\nsection ->  [startIndex, startIndex + span)\n```\n\nA 2-span section owns two consecutive stops, both inside the same continuous element. Nothing\nin the DOM has to be split or measured to know that.\n\nBecause every stop is an integer multiple of `stride`, both the native CSS snap points and the\nJS index math are pure arithmetic:\n\n- **Snapping** is native: `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` on the track, `scroll-snap-stop: always`\n  plus `scroll-snap-align: start` on each section, and one invisible 1px anchor per extra unit of\n  a wide section. One gesture travels one stride; the browser keeps momentum, touch physics and\n  accessibility.\n- **Position** is derived, not observed: `index = round((scrollLeft - pad) / stride)`, and the\n  active section is whichever `[start, start + span)` contains that index. No\n  `getBoundingClientRect` scans over section children.\n\n## Components\n\n```\n<Panorama @section-change=\"onSectionChange\" ref=\"pano\">\n  <PanoramaSection id=\"about\" title=\"about\">...anything...</PanoramaSection>\n  <PanoramaSection id=\"projects\" title=\"projects\" :span=\"2\">...anything...</PanoramaSection>\n  <PanoramaSection id=\"experience\" title=\"experience\">...anything...</PanoramaSection>\n</Panorama>\n```\n\n`Panorama` owns the track, the stop table, index tracking and programmatic scrolling:\n\n| Member                                | Description                                                          |\n| ------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `scrollToSection(id, { lead?: number })` | scroll a section's start into view; `lead` lands `lead` stops earlier |\n| `scrollToIndex(i)`                    | scroll to an absolute stop                                            |\n| `activeIndex` / `activeSection`       | current stop and owning section id (reactive)                         |\n| `totalStops`                          | number of stops in the track                                          |\n| `@section-change`                     | emitted when the active section id changes                            |\n| `@index-change`                       | emitted when the active stop changes                                  |\n\n`PanoramaSection` declares `id`, optional `title`/header slot, `span` (integer, default 1) and\n`spanSm` (span from 640px up, optional). It registers itself with the parent and renders its own\nwidth, its snap anchors and an optional internally scrolling body. `span` is deliberately not\nreactive to content: if a section needs more room, change the span.\n\nKeyboard: the track is focusable and `ArrowLeft` / `ArrowRight` step exactly one stop.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. Content never reports its width upward. The framework never adapts to content; content adapts\n   inside a fixed span.\n2. `span` is a positive integer. Fractional spans have no valid stop grid.\n3. Wide sections are continuous. If content needs sub-sections, that is the content's own\n   layout, not the framework's.\n4. Vertical overflow is the section's business (`scrollable` body), not the framework's.\n5. Reuse the components instead of re-implementing a scroller - the project detail view is the\n   same framework with one section per panel.\n"
}
````

> _Reasoning_
> Also `.snap-anchor` in PanoramaView: `position:absolute; top:0; bottom:0; width:1px; left via style`. Fine.
>

> 
> Now the framework. Create directory files:
> - `src/components/panorama/framework/panorama-context.ts`
> - `src/components/panorama/framework/Panorama.vue`
> - `src/components/panorama/framework/PanoramaSection.vue`
> 
> Check ESLint config for naming conventions and prettier style (no semicolons, 4 spaces, single quotes). Follow existing.
> 
> panorama-context.ts:
> 
> ```ts
> import type { ComputedRef, InjectionKey } from 'vue'
> 
> export interface PanoramaRegistration {
>     id: string
>     getSpan: () => number
> }
> 
> export interface PanoramaContext {
>     register: (entry: PanoramaRegistration) => void
>     unregister: (id: string) => void
> }
> 
> export const PANORAMA_CONTEXT: InjectionKey<PanoramaContext> = Symbol('panorama')
> ```
> 
> Panorama.vue script:
> 
> ```ts
> import { computed, nextTick, onMounted, onUnmounted, provide, ref, watch } from 'vue'
> import { PANORAMA_CONTEXT, type PanoramaRegistration } from './panorama-context'
> 
> const emit = defineEmits<{
>     'section-change': [id: string]
>     'index-change': [index: number]
> }>()
> 
> const track = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)
> const registrations = ref<PanoramaRegistration[]>([])
> const scrollLeft = ref(0)
> const metrics = ref({ stride: 0, pad: 0 })
> 
> let frame: number | null = null
> let resizeObserver: ResizeObserver | null = null
> 
> const register = (entry) => { ... }
> ```
> 
> For registering order: sections mount in order, push. Unregister removes. Fine.
> 
> stop table:
> ```ts
> const stops = computed(() => {
>     let cursor = 0
>     return registrations.value.map((entry) => {
>         const span = Math.max(1, Math.round(entry.getSpan()))
>         const stop = { id: entry.id, span, start: cursor }
>         cursor += span
>         return stop
>     })
> })
> const totalStops = computed(() => stops.value.reduce((total, stop) => total + stop.span, 0))
> ```
> Note: `.getSpan()` reading reactive props inside computed → tracked. Good.
> 
> measure:
> ```ts
> const measure = () => {
>     const el = track.value
>     if (!el) return
>     const pad = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(el).scrollPaddingLeft)
>     const first = el.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[data-pano-section]')
>     if (!first) { metrics.value = { stride: 0, pad: pad || 0 }; return }
>     const gap = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(first).marginRight) || 0
>     const span = Math.max(1, Number.parseInt(first.dataset.span ?? '1', 10) || 1)
>     const unit = (first.getBoundingClientRect().width - (span - 1) * gap) / span
>     metrics.value = { stride: unit + gap, pad: Number.isFinite(pad) ? pad : 0 }
> }
> ```
> 
> index:
> ```ts
> const clamp = (value, min, max) => Math.min(Math.max(value, min), max)
> const activeIndex = computed(() => {
>     const { stride, pad } = metrics.value
>     if (stride <= 0 || totalStops.value === 0) return 0
>     return clamp(Math.round((scrollLeft.value - pad) / stride), 0, totalStops.value - 1)
> })
> const activeSection = computed(() => stops.value.find(... )?.id ?? '')
> ```
> 
> watch activeSection → emit; watch activeIndex → emit index-change.
> 
> scroll handler:
> ```ts
> const syncScroll = () => {
>     if (frame !== null) return
>     frame = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
>         frame = null
>         scrollLeft.value = track.value?.scrollLeft ?? 0
>     })
> }
> ```
> 
> scrollToIndex:
> ```ts
> const scrollToIndex = (index: number) => {
>     const el = track.value
>     const { stride, pad } = metrics.value
>     if (!el || stride <= 0) return
>     el.scrollTo({
>         left: clamp(index, 0, Math.max(0, totalStops.value - 1)) * stride + pad,
>         behavior: prefersReducedMotion() ? 'auto' : 'smooth',
>     })
> }
> const scrollToSection = (id: string, options: { lead?: number } = {}) => {
>     const stop = stops.value.find((item) => item.id === id)
>     if (!stop) return
>     scrollToIndex(stop.start - (options.lead ?? 0))
> }
> ```
> 
> prefersReducedMotion: `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches`.
> 
> keyboard:
> ```ts
> const onKeydown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
>     if (event.key === 'ArrowRight') { event.preventDefault(); scrollToIndex(activeIndex.value + 1) }
>     else if (event.key === 'ArrowLeft') { ... -1 }
> }
> ```
> 
> mount: measure, listeners scroll/resize, ResizeObserver on inner (children width changes e.g. project tile measurement). Also after registration changes: `watch(totalStops, () => nextTick(measure))`.
> 
> Provide context.
> 
> defineExpose({ scrollToSection, scrollToIndex, activeIndex, activeSection, totalStops }).
> 
> Template:
> ```vue
> <main
>     ref="track"
>     class="pano-track"
>     tabindex="0"
>     @scroll.passive="syncScroll"
>     @keydown="onKeydown"
> >
>     <div ref="inner" class="pano-inner">
>         <slot />
>         <div class="pano-trailing" aria-hidden="true" />
>     </div>
> </main>
> ```
> scroll event with passive: Vue `@scroll.passive`.
> 
> Styles in Panorama.vue (scoped, but .pano-inner children? sections are child components — scoped styles won't apply to their internals, but section styles are in PanoramaSection's own scoped block; fine).
> 
> ```css
> .pano-track {
>     --pano-unit: 50vw;
>     --pano-gap: 4rem;
>     --pano-pad: 8rem;
>     --pano-stride: calc(var(--pano-unit) + var(--pano-gap));
> 
>     position: relative;
>     overflow-x: auto;
>     overflow-y: hidden;
>     scroll-snap-type: x mandatory;
>     scroll-padding-left: var(--pano-pad);
>     scroll-padding-right: var(--pano-pad);
>     overscroll-behavior-x: contain;

> Anchors count = max(base, sm ?? base).

So no media hiding needed. Remove it.

```json
{
  "path": "src/components/panorama/framework/Panorama.vue",
  "instructions": "Generic panorama track: derives snap stops from section spans and scrolls by stop index.",
  "content": "<script setup lang=\"ts\">\nimport { computed, nextTick, onMounted, onUnmounted, provide, ref, watch } from 'vue'\nimport { PANORAMA_CONTEXT, type PanoramaRegistration } from './panorama-context'\n\nconst emit = defineEmits<{\n    'section-change': [id: string]\n    'index-change': [index: number]\n}>()\n\nconst track = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)\nconst registrations = ref<PanoramaRegistration[]>([])\nconst scrollLeft = ref(0)\nconst metrics = ref({ stride: 0, pad: 0 })\n\nlet frame: number | null = null\nlet resizeObserver: ResizeObserver | null = null\n\nconst clamp = (value: number, min: number, max: number) => Math.min(Math.max(value, min), max)\n\n/* Sections own their span; the framework only accumulates it into stops. */\nconst register = (entry: PanoramaRegistration) => {\n    if (!registrations.value.some((item) => item.id === entry.id)) {\n        registrations.value.push(entry)\n    }\n}\n\nconst unregister = (id: string) => {\n    registrations.value = registrations.value.filter((item) => item.id !== id)\n}\n\nprovide(PANORAMA_CONTEXT, { register, unregister })\n\nconst stops = computed(() => {\n    let cursor = 0\n    return registrations.value.map((entry) => {\n        const span = Math.max(1, Math.round(entry.getSpan()))\n        const stop = { id: entry.id, span, start: cursor }\n        cursor += span\n        return stop\n    })\n})\n\nconst totalStops = computed(() => stops.value.reduce((total, stop) => total + stop.span, 0))\n\n/* One measure pass reads the standard metrics off the CSS, never content width. */\nconst measure = () => {\n    const el = track.value\n    if (!el) return\n\n    const pad = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(el).scrollPaddingLeft) || 0\n    const first = el.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[data-pano-section]')\n\n    if (!first) {\n        metrics.value = { stride: 0, pad }\n        return\n    }\n\n    const gap = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(first).marginRight) || 0\n    const span = Math.max(1, Number.parseInt(first.dataset.span ?? '1', 10) || 1)\n    const unit = (first.getBoundingClientRect().width - (span - 1) * gap) / span\n\n    metrics.value = { stride: unit + gap, pad }\n}\n\nconst syncScroll = () => {\n    if (frame !== null) return\n    frame = requestAnimationFrame(() => {\n        frame = null\n        scrollLeft.value = track.value?.scrollLeft ?? 0\n    })\n}\n\nconst prefersReducedMotion = () =>\n    typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches\n\nconst scrollToIndex = (index: number) => {\n    const el = track.value\n    const { stride, pad } = metrics.value\n    if (!el || stride <= 0) return\n\n    el.scrollTo({\n        left: clamp(index, 0, Math.max(0, totalStops.value - 1)) * stride + pad,\n        behavior: prefersReducedMotion() ? 'auto' : 'smooth',\n    })\n}\n\nconst scrollToSection = (id: string, options: { lead?: number } = {}) => {\n    const stop = stops.value.find((item) => item.id === id)\n    if (!stop) return\n    scrollToIndex(stop.start - (options.lead ?? 0))\n}\n\nconst activeIndex = computed(() => {\n    const { stride, pad } = metrics.value\n    if (stride <= 0 || totalStops.value === 0) return 0\n    return clamp(Math.round((scrollLeft.value - pad) / stride), 0, totalStops.value - 1)\n})\n\nconst activeSection = computed(\n    () =>\n        stops.value.find(\n            (stop) => activeIndex.value >= stop.start && activeIndex.value < stop.start + stop.span,\n        )?.id ?? '',\n)\n\nconst onKeydown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {\n    if (event.key === 'ArrowRight') {\n        event.preventDefault()\n        scrollToIndex(activeIndex.value + 1)\n    } else if (event.key === 'ArrowLeft') {\n        event.preventDefault()\n        scrollToIndex(activeIndex.value - 1)\n    }\n}\n\nwatch(activeSection, (id) => {\n    if (id) emit('section-change', id)\n})\n\nwatch(activeIndex, (index) => emit('index-change', index))\n\nwatch(totalStops, () => nextTick(measure))\n\nonMounted(() => {\n    measure()\n    window.addEventListener('resize', measure)\n\n    if (track.value?.firstElementChild) {\n        resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver(measure)\n        resizeObserver.observe(track.value.firstElementChild)\n    }\n})\n\nonUnmounted(() => {\n    window.removeEventListener('resize', measure)\n    resizeObserver?.disconnect()\n    if (frame !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(frame)\n})\n\ndefineExpose({ scrollToSection, scrollToIndex, activeIndex, activeSection, totalStops })\n</script>\n\n<template>\n    <main\n        ref=\"track\"\n        class=\"pano-track\"\n        tabindex=\"0\"\n        @scroll.passive=\"syncScroll\"\n        @keydown=\"onKeydown\"\n    >\n        <div class=\"pano-inner\">\n            <slot />\n            <div class=\"pano-trailing\" aria-hidden=\"true\" />\n        </div>\n    </main>\n</template>\n\n<style scoped>\n/*\n * Panorama metrics. One regular section is --pano-unit wide, neighbors are\n * --pano-gap apart, and one tick travels --pano-stride. Override these tokens\n * on the track to change the rhythm; consumers can size the track itself.\n */\n.pano-track {\n    --pano-unit: 50vw;\n    --pano-gap: 4rem;\n    --pano-pad: 8rem;\n    --pano-stride: calc(var(--pano-unit) + var(--pano-gap));\n\n    position: relative;\n    overflow-x: auto;\n    overflow-y: hidden;\n    scroll-snap-type: x mandatory;\n    scroll-padding-left: var(--pano-pad);\n    scroll-padding-right: var(--pano-pad);\n    overscroll-behavior-x: contain;\n    overscroll-behavior-y: contain;\n    -webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch;\n    outline: none;\n}\n\n@media (max-width: 639px) {\n    .pano-track {\n        --pano-unit: 85vw;\n        --pano-gap: 1rem;\n        --pano-pad: 1.5rem;\n    }\n}\n\n.pano-inner {\n    display: flex;\n    align-items: stretch;\n    width: max-content;\n    height: 100%;\n    padding-left: var(--pano-pad);\n}\n\n/* Lets the last stop align to the leading padding instead of being clamped. */\n.pano-trailing {\n    flex: none;\n    width: max(0px, calc(100vw - var(--pano-pad) - var(--pano-unit)));\n}\n</style>\n"
}
```

{
  "path": "src/components/panorama/framework/PanoramaSection.vue",
  "instructions": "Generic panorama section declaring an integer span in regular-section units.",
  "content": "<script setup lang=\"ts\">\nimport { computed, inject, onMounted, onUnmounted, ref } from 'vue'\nimport { PANORAMA_CONTEXT } from './panorama-context'\n\nconst props = withDefaults(\n    defineProps<{\n        id: string\n        title?: string\n        /** Width in regular-section units. Integer >= 1. */\n        span?: number\n        /** Span to use from the 640px breakpoint up, if it differs. */\n        spanSm?: number\n        /** Let the body scroll vertically when its content is taller than the section. */\n        scrollable?: boolean\n    }>(),\n    { span: 1, spanSm: undefined, title: undefined, scrollable: false },\n)\n\nconst context = inject(PANORAMA_CONTEXT, null)\n\nconst wide = ref(false)\nlet query: MediaQueryList | null = null\n\nconst syncWide = () => {\n    wide.value = query?.matches ?? false\n}\n\nconst baseSpan = computed(() => Math.max(1, Math.round(props.span)))\nconst smSpan = computed(() => (props.spanSm ? Math.max(1, Math.round(props.spanSm)) : baseSpan.value))\n\n/** Registered so the parent can build its stop table; reads reactive state. */\nconst getSpan = () => (wide.value ? smSpan.value : baseSpan.value)\n\n/** Anchors cover the largest span seen at any breakpoint; duplicates are harmless. */\nconst anchorCount = computed(() => Math.max(baseSpan.value, smSpan.value))\n\ncontext?.register({ id: props.id, getSpan })\n\nonMounted(() => {\n    query = window.matchMedia('(min-width: 640px)')\n    syncWide()\n    query.addEventListener('change', syncWide)\n})\n\nonUnmounted(() => {\n    query?.removeEventListener('change', syncWide)\n    context?.unregister(props.id)\n})\n</script>\n\n<template>\n    <section\n        :id=\"props.id\"\n        data-pano-section\n        :data-span=\"baseSpan\"\n        class=\"pano-section\"\n        :style=\"{\n            '--pano-span': baseSpan,\n            '--pano-span-sm': props.spanSm ? smSpan : undefined,\n        }\"\n    >\n        <div v-if=\"props.title || $slots.header\" class=\"pano-section-header\">\n            <slot name=\"header\">\n                <h2 class=\"pano-section-title\">{{ props.title }}</h2>\n            </slot>\n        </div>\n\n        <div class=\"pano-section-body\" :class=\"{ 'pano-section-body--scroll': props.scrollable }\">\n            <slot />\n        </div>\n\n        <div\n            v-for=\"anchor in anchorCount - 1\"\n            :key=\"anchor\"\n            class=\"pano-anchor\"\n            :style=\"{ left: `calc(var(--pano-stride) * ${anchor})` }\"\n            aria-hidden=\"true\"\n        />\n    </section>\n</template>\n\n<style scoped>\n.pano-section {\n    --pano-span-current: var(--pano-span, 1);\n\n    position: relative;\n    flex: none;\n    height: 100%;\n    display: flex;\n    flex-direction: column;\n    width: calc(\n        var(--pano-unit) * var(--pano-span-current) + var(--pano-gap) *\n            (var(--pano-span-current) - 1)\n    );\n    margin-right: var(--pano-gap);\n    scroll-snap-align: start;\n    scroll-snap-stop: always;\n}\n\n@media (min-width: 640px) {\n    .pano-section {\n        --pano-span-current: var(--pano-span-sm, var(--pano-span, 1));\n    }\n}\n\n.pano-section-header {\n    flex: none;\n}\n\n.pano-section-body {\n    flex: 1 1 auto;\n    min-height: 0;\n    display: flex;\n    flex-direction: column;\n}\n\n.pano-section-body--scroll {\n    overflow-y: auto;\n    overscroll-behavior-y: contain;\n}\n\n/*\n * Extra snap stops inside a wide section. Absolutely positioned so the section\n * stays one continuous box: no layout, no gaps, no nested containers.\n */\n.pano-anchor {\n    position: absolute;\n    top: 0;\n    bottom: 0;\n    width: 1px;\n    pointer-events: none;\n    scroll-snap-align: start;\n    scroll-snap-stop: always;\n}\n</style>\n"
}

> Should I sanity-test the framework logic? Without a browser, I can't. But I could at least instantiate in a small unit test? No test infra. Report limitation.
> 
> One concern: `watch(totalStops, ...)` — totalStops recomputes when `wide` changes, but does `registrations`/`getSpan` reactivity propagate? `stops` computed calls `entry.getSpan()` which reads `wide.value` and `props.span` → tracked. Yes.
> 
> Another: `measure()` runs on mount before sections' `wide` is resolved; stride derived from first section's DOM width — correct regardless.
> 
> Potential issue: PanoramaSection register happens in setup before mount; parent's `measure` in onMounted of parent runs before child mounted? Parent's onMounted runs after children mounted, so DOM exists. Good.
> 
> Potential issue: `first.dataset.span` is base span (not sm). On desktop with spanSm=2 and base=1, the first section's actual width is 2 units; dataset.span=1 → unit computed = width = 2u+g, overestimating stride by u+g. Bug! I set `:data-span="baseSpan"` — should be the effective span at current breakpoint. In PanoramaView project case: span=2 always (no spanSm), so fine, but for generic correctness fix: `:data-span="effectiveSpan"` where effectiveSpan = wide ? smSpan : baseSpan. But that's JS state; measure() re-runs on resize (breakpoint change triggers? window resize listener yes; matchMedia change doesn't necessarily fire resize). Better: `data-span` as a CSS-set value? Can't read computed custom property as number easily... Simplest: bind dataset to `wide ? smSpan : baseSpan`, and have parent watch... parent measures on resize; crossing 640px mid-window is always a resize event. Also matchMedia change coincides with resize. Acceptable.
> 
> Alternatively derive unit without dataset: measure second section's left minus first's left minus gap? That gives first width + gap; unit = (w1+g - g)= w1 → wrong for span.
> 
> Better robust approach: compute stride from the *anchor grid*, which is guaranteed stride-spaced: if a section has an anchor, stride = anchor.getBoundingClientRect().left - section.left. For all-span-1 tracks there are no anchors. Hmm.
> 
> Use dataset effective span; add a watcher in section to update `data-span`? Binding expression recomputes reactively: `:data-span="wide ? smSpan : baseSpan"` — Vue updates the attribute when `wide` changes. Then parent needs to re-measure: it only listens to window resize, not matchMedia. But crossing breakpoint implies resize in practice. Also parent's ResizeObserver on the inner element fires when section widths change (CSS width changes) → measure re-runs and reads updated dataset? Attribute update and RO callback ordering: RO fires after layout; Vue patch is sync in watcher flush (microtask/pre-render). Probably dataset updated before RO callback (RO delivered after render usually). Acceptable.
> 
> Hmm, but ResizeObserver on firstElementChild (the inner div) — its content-box width changes when section width changes at breakpoint, so yes it fires. Good.
> 
> Let me change data-span binding to effective span. Also apply to PanoramaView's `.snap-wide`? Not needed (fixed 2).
> 
> Also: `anchorCount` anchors rendering with `left: calc(var(--pano-stride) * n)` — for spanSm=2 base=1 on mobile: anchor at stride exists but section width=1 unit → anchor sits exactly at next section's start. Duplicate stop, harmless as reasoned.
> 
> Additional check: `width` calc line breaks prettier formatting; build fine.

Steps 1–2 are in place, plus the doc.

**`docs/panorama-framework.md`** — the contract: metrics table, the `unit × n + gap × (n−1)` width formula, stop math (`x = pad + i·stride`), the component/API surface, and the five rules (content never reports width; integer spans; wide sections stay continuous; vertical overflow is the section's business; reuse the components).

**Step 1 — metrics as tokens (`PanoramaView.vue`)**
- One token block on `.panorama-track`: `--pano-unit` (85vw → 50vw), `--pano-gap` (1rem → 4rem), `--pano-pad` (1.5rem → 8rem), `--pano-stride`.
- `.snap-section` now takes `--pano-span: 1` and derives width from the formula; `.snap-wide` just sets `--pano-span: 2`. The per-breakpoint `50vw/100vw/85vw` literals and the mobile width override block are gone.
- `max-width` hacks removed; `scroll-padding` and the old `pl-6 sm:pl-32` now read the same `--pano-pad`.
- The `#projects-experience-view` anchor moved from `left: 50%` to `left: var(--pano-stride)` — it now lands exactly on stop 2 of the 2-span section instead of drifting 4rem off the grid.

**Step 2 — generic components (`src/components/panorama/framework/`)**
- `panorama-context.ts`: injection key; sections register `{ id, getSpan }` so the parent builds the stop table without a parallel spans array.
- `Panorama.vue`: track + metrics, `stops`/`totalStops` accumulated from registered spans, `index = round((scrollLeft − pad) / stride)` with per-section `[start, start+span)` resolution, `scrollToSection(id, { lead })`, `scrollToIndex`, `scrollToSection`/`activeIndex`/`activeSection` exposed, `section-change` + `index-change` emits, ArrowLeft/Right stepping exactly one stop, trailing spacer so the last stop aligns to the padding, and reduced-motion respected. Measurement reads the standard metrics off the first section's box and `margin-right` — never content.
- `PanoramaSection.vue`: `id`/`title`/`span`/`spanSm`/`scrollable`, width from the same formula via `--pano-span`, and one absolutely-positioned 1px `scroll-snap-align: start` anchor per extra unit — so a wide section owns multiple stops while remaining a single continuous box with no internal gaps.

Verification: `npm run build` (vue-tsc `-b` + vite) passes, `npx eslint` on the changed framework files is clean. Not verified: actual snapping/gesture behavior — no browser is available here, and the components aren't wired into a view yet (they're tree-shaken out, which is why the bundle hash is unchanged). That check comes with step 3.

Two notes for when you pick this up: `PanoramaView` now has its own copy of the token block, which step 3 removes (it becomes the framework's track); and the projects section still supplies its measured pixel width inline, so it's currently `~64px` narrower than its declared 2-unit span — that goes away in step 4 when `widthChange` is dropped.

- path: src\components\panorama\PanoramaView.vue
  content: |
      <script setup lang="ts">
      import { computed, ref, onMounted, onUnmounted } from 'vue'
      import type { Experience, Project, Skill } from '@/types'
      import PanoramaIntro from './PanoramaIntro.vue'
      import PanoramaProjects from './PanoramaProjects.vue'
      import PanoramaExperience from './PanoramaExperience.vue'
      import PanoramaContact from './PanoramaContact.vue'
      import PanoramaProjectDetail from './PanoramaProjectDetail.vue'
      
      const props = defineProps<{
          skills: Skill[]
          experience: Experience[]
          projects: Project[]
          copyBtnText: string
      }>()
      
      const emit = defineEmits<{
          copy: []
          sectionChange: [section: 'about' | 'projects' | 'experience' | 'contact']
      }>()
      
      const viewport = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)
      const selectedProject = ref<Project | null>(null)
      const projectsWidth = ref(0)
      const lastSection = ref<'about' | 'projects' | 'experience' | 'contact'>('about')
      
      const sections = computed(() => [
          { id: 'about', title: 'about', vertical: true, component: 'intro' as const },
          { id: 'projects', title: 'projects', vertical: false, component: 'projects' as const },
          { id: 'experience', title: 'experience', vertical: true, component: 'experience' as const },
          { id: 'contact', title: 'contact', vertical: false, component: 'contact' as const },
      ])
      
      const updateActiveSection = () => {
          const el = viewport.value
          if (!el) return
          const rect = el.getBoundingClientRect()
          const mid = rect.left + rect.width / 2
          let active: typeof lastSection.value = 'about'
          for (const sec of sections.value) {
              const node = el.querySelector<HTMLElement>(`#${sec.id}`)
              if (!node) continue
              const r = node.getBoundingClientRect()
              if (r.left <= mid && r.right >= mid) {
                  active = sec.component === 'intro' ? 'about'
                      : sec.component === 'projects' ? 'projects'
                      : sec.component === 'experience' ? 'experience'
                      : 'contact'
                  break
              }
          }
          if (active !== lastSection.value) {
              lastSection.value = active
              emit('sectionChange', active)
          }
      }
      
      let scrollTimer: ReturnType<typeof requestAnimationFrame> | null = null
      const onScroll = () => {
          if (scrollTimer) return
          scrollTimer = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
              scrollTimer = null
              updateActiveSection()
          })
      }
      
      const navigateToSection = (section: 'about' | 'projects' | 'experience' | 'contact') => {
          const track = viewport.value
          if (!track) return
      
          // For experience, scroll to the projects snap-anchor to show both projects (right half) and experience
          const targetId = section === 'experience' ? 'projects-experience-view' : section
          const node = track.querySelector<HTMLElement>(`#${targetId}`)
          if (!node) return
      
          const gutter = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(track).scrollPaddingLeft) || 0
          const target =
              node.getBoundingClientRect().left -
              track.getBoundingClientRect().left +
              track.scrollLeft -
              gutter
      
          track.scrollTo({ left: Math.max(0, target), behavior: 'smooth' })
      }
      
      const openProject = (project: Project) => {
          selectedProject.value = project
      }
      
      const closeProject = () => {
          selectedProject.value = null
      }
      
      const sectionWidth = (id: string) =>
          id === 'projects' && projectsWidth.value > 0 ? `${projectsWidth.value}px` : undefined
      
      defineExpose({ scrollToSection: navigateToSection })
      
      onMounted(() => {
          if (viewport.value) {
              viewport.value.addEventListener('scroll', onScroll, { passive: true })
              updateActiveSection()
          }
      })
      
      onUnmounted(() => {
          if (viewport.value) viewport.value.removeEventListener('scroll', onScroll)
          if (scrollTimer) cancelAnimationFrame(scrollTimer)
      })
      </script>
      
      <template>
          <div
              id="app"
              class="h-screen w-screen flex flex-col bg-white text-black overflow-hidden select-none dark:bg-zinc-950 dark:text-white"
          >
              <main
                  ref="viewport"
                  class="panorama-track flex-1 min-h-0 w-full overflow-x-auto overflow-y-hidden relative"
                  tabindex="0"
              >
                  <div class="track-inner flex items-stretch h-full pt-6 pb-8">
                      <section
                          v-for="section in sections"
                          :id="section.id"
                          :key="section.id"
                          class="snap-section flex-none flex flex-col"
                          :class="section.id === 'projects' ? 'snap-wide' : ''"
                          :style="{ width: sectionWidth(section.id) }"
                      >
      
      [read_files: showing lines 1-130 of 269. Use code_search to locate the part you need and read a window around it, or call read_files again with offset=131 to continue.]
```

                                  v-else-if="section.component === 'contact'"
                                  :copy-btn-text="props.copyBtnText"
                                  @copy="emit('copy')"
                              />
                          </div>
      
                          <div
                              v-if="section.id === 'projects'"
                              id="projects-experience-view"
                              class="snap-anchor"
                              :style="{ left: 'var(--pano-stride)' }"
                              aria-hidden="true"
                          />
                      </section>
                  </div>
              </main>
      
              <Transition name="panorama-swap" mode="out-in">
                  <PanoramaProjectDetail
                      v-if="selectedProject"
                      key="detail"
                      :project="selectedProject"
                      @back="closeProject"
                  />
              </Transition>
          </div>
      </template>
      
      <style scoped>
      /*
       * Panorama metrics: one regular section is --pano-unit wide, sections are separated
       * by --pano-gap, and one tick/gesture travels --pano-stride. A span-n section is
       * n units plus the n-1 gaps it swallows, so every stop is a multiple of the stride.
       */
      .panorama-track {
          --pano-unit: 85vw;
          --pano-gap: 1rem;
          --pano-pad: 1.5rem;
          --pano-stride: calc(var(--pano-unit) + var(--pano-gap));
      
          scroll-snap-type: x mandatory;
          scroll-padding-left: var(--pano-pad);
          scroll-padding-right: var(--pano-pad);
          overscroll-behavior-x: contain;
          -webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch;
      }
      
      @media (min-width: 640px) {
          .panorama-track {
              --pano-unit: 50vw;
              --pano-gap: 4rem;
              --pano-pad: 8rem;
          }
      }
      
      .track-inner {
          height: 100%;
          width: max-content;
          display: flex;
          align-items: stretch;
          padding-left: var(--pano-pad);
      }
      
      .snap-section {
          --pano-span: 1;
      
          scroll-snap-align: start;
          scroll-snap-stop: always;
          position: relative;
          width: calc(var(--pano-unit) * var(--pano-span) + var(--pano-gap) * (var(--pano-span) - 1));
          margin-right: var(--pano-gap);
          height: 100%;
          display: flex;
          flex-direction: column;
      }
      
      .snap-section.snap-wide {
          --pano-span: 2;
      }
      
      .snap-anchor {
          scroll-snap-align: start;
          scroll-snap-stop: always;
          position: absolute;
          top: 0;

      .panorama-swap-leave-active {

      .panorama-swap-leave-to {

'about'\r\n                : sec.component === 'projects' ? 'projects'\r\n                : sec.component === 'experience' ? 'experience'\r\n                : 'contact'\r\n            break\r\n        }\r\n    }\r\n    if (active !== lastSection.value) {\r\n        lastSection.value = active\r\n        emit('sectionChange', active)\r\n    }\r\n}\r\n\r\nlet scrollTimer: ReturnType<typeof requestAnimationFrame> | null = null\r\nconst onScroll = () => {\r\n    if (scrollTimer) return\r\n    scrollTimer = requestAnimationFrame(() => {\r\n        scrollTimer = null\r\n        updateActiveSection()\r\n    })\r\n}\r\n\r\nconst navigateToSection = (section: 'about' | 'projects' | 'experience' | 'contact') => {\r\n    const track = viewport.value\r\n    if (!track) return\r\n\r\n    // For experience, scroll to the projects snap-anchor to show both projects (right half) and experience\r\n    const targetId = section === 'experience' ? 'projects-experience-view' : section\r\n    const node = track.querySelector<HTMLElement>(`#${targetId}`)\r\n    if (!node) return\r\n\r\n    const gutter = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(track).scrollPaddingLeft) || 0\r\n    const target =\r\n        node.getBoundingClientRect().left -\r\n        track.getBoundingClientRect().left +\r\n        track.scrollLeft -\r\n        gutter\r\n\r\n    track.scrollTo({ left: Math.max(0, target), behavior: 'smooth' })\r\n}",

      }>()

          copy: []
          sectionChange: [section: SectionId]
      }>()
      
      const panorama = ref<InstanceType<typeof Panorama> | null>(null)
      const selectedProject = ref<Project | null>(null)
      
      const sections = computed(() => [
          { id: 'about' as const, title: 'about', span: 1, vertical: true, component: 'intro' as const },
          {
              id: 'projects' as const,
              title: 'projects',
              span: 2,

              title: 'contact',
              span: 1,
              vertical: false,
              component: 'contact' as const,
          },

      /**
       * Section -> stop. `experience` leads by one stop so the projects section stays
       * partly visible; that used to be a hand-placed anchor inside the wide section.
       */
      const navigateToSection = (section: SectionId) => {
          panorama.value?.scrollToSection(section, { lead: section === 'experience' ? 1 : 0 })
      }
      
      const onSectionChange = (id: string) => emit('sectionChange', id as SectionId)

          selectedProject.value = project
      }

          selectedProject.value = null
      }

      <script setup lang="ts">
      import { computed, onMounted, onUnmounted, ref } from 'vue'
      import type { Project } from '@/types'
      import PanoramaProjectTile from './PanoramaProjectTile.vue'
      
      const props = defineProps<{
          projects: Project[]
      }>()
      
      const emit = defineEmits<{
          select: [project: Project]
      }>()
      
      const GAP = 16
      const COLUMNS = 5
      const MOBILE_COLUMNS = 2
      const PREVIOUS_COLUMNS = 6
      const MAX_TILE_WIDTH = 400
      const MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 640
      
      const container = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)
      const containerHeight = ref(0)
      const containerWidth = ref(0)
      const viewportWidth = ref(window.innerWidth)
      
      let resizeObserver: ResizeObserver | null = null
      
      const updateViewportWidth = () => {
          viewportWidth.value = window.innerWidth
      }
      
      onMounted(() => {
          updateViewportWidth()
          window.addEventListener('resize', updateViewportWidth)
      
          // Size the grid from the section box the framework declared, never from the viewport.
          resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver((entries) => {
              const entry = entries[0]
              if (!entry) return
              containerHeight.value = entry.contentRect.height
              containerWidth.value = entry.contentRect.width
          })
      
          if (container.value) resizeObserver.observe(container.value)
      })

**Now live in panorama mode**
- `PanoramaView.vue` renders `<Panorama>` / `<PanoramaSection>`; its own token block, `.snap-section` / `.snap-wide`, the `#projects-experience-view` anchor, the scroll listener and the `getBoundingClientRect` active-section scan are all gone (~115 lines lighter). - Nav uses `scrollToSection(id)`; the "show the right half of projects plus experience" behaviour is now `scrollToSection('experience', { lead: 1 })` instead of a hand-placed 1px anchor.
- Header highlighting comes from the framework's `section-change`, derived from `round((scrollLeft − pad) / stride)`.
- `PanoramaProjects` no longer measures itself and reports width upward: `widthChange` and `VIEWPORT_GUTTER` are gone, and the grid sizes from its own `ResizeObserver` box — so the wide section is exactly two units and content reflows inside it.
- Removed the now-dead global `.panorama-track` / `.snap-section` / `.snap-anchor` rules from `main.css`.

**Bug caught by checking the math**
The trailing spacer was `100vw − pad − unit`, 64px short at desktop, so the last stop could never align (scroll clamped). Required slack is `100vw − stride`. At 1440px the grid now checks out: unit 720, gap 64, stride 784, pad 128 → section starts at 128 / 912 / 2480 / 3264 = `pad + i·stride`, content 4640, max scroll 3200+… exactly reaching the final stop.

**Verification**: `npm run build` (vue-tsc + vite) passes, ESLint clean on the framework, view and projects files. Not verified: actual gesture snapping, active-highlight behaviour and the wide section's visual width in a browser — no browser is available in this environment, so that needs a `npm run dev` look on your side. The project detail view is still the old `panorama-scroll` markup (free scrolling, no snap) — porting it is the remaining step.
