And the detail view still uses its own scroller — not framework-aided yet.

Yes — three pieces of project-specific code were quietly doing the framework's job. All three are now gone.

**1. Content measured itself and pushed its width up**
`PanoramaProjects` computed `gridWidth` from the project list (columns × tiles + gaps) and emitted `widthChange`; `PanoramaView` turned that into an inline `width` on the projects section. So the wide section's size came from counting projects, not from a declared span — add a project and the snap grid moved. Worse, its `tileWidth` used `window.innerWidth − VIEWPORT_GUTTER`, encoding the panorama's layout metric *inside content*. Now the section is `span: 2` from CSS and the grid reflows from its own `ResizeObserver` box.

**2. A named 1px div standing in for "stop 2 of a 2-span section"**
`#projects-experience-view` at `left: 50%` existed purely so clicking "experience" would land mid-wide-section and show projects on the left. The framework had no notion that a wide section owns multiple stops, so the view faked one, with the section id baked into the name. Now it's `scrollToSection('experience', { lead: 1 })`, and every wide section gets its stops from `span`.

**3. The template knew which section was wide**
`section.id === 'projects' ? 'snap-wide' : ''` plus a `.snap-wide { width: 100vw }` rule — the wideness was keyed to one hardcoded id and a viewport literal that didn't match the gap-and-unit rhythm. Now `--pano-span` comes from the section's own `span` prop.

Also panorama-specific but not project-specific, and similarly removed: the per-view token block, the `component` string → section-id mapping in the active-section scan, the `vertical` flag threaded through the template, and the duplicated `.panorama-track` / `.snap-section` rules in `main.css`.

What's left in `PanoramaView` that you can still call project-specific is *configuration*, not framework aid: the four section ids, `projects` having `span: 2`, `experience` using `lead: 1`, and the `:deep(.pano-inner)` vertical padding. That's exactly what a new project would edit. The project **detail** view is the one place still outside the framework — it uses `.panorama-scroll` / `.panorama-panel` and doesn't snap at all.

Close, but I'd call it "copy-three-files with two sharp edges" rather than fully plug-and-play. Honest status:

**What is genuinely portable**
`framework/Panorama.vue`, `framework/PanoramaSection.vue`, `panorama-context.ts` — ~200 lines, importing nothing but `vue` (no `@/`, no project data, no component names). Drop the folder in, declare sections with `:span`, done.

**Edge 1 — the rhythm tokens aren't overridable from outside**
`.pano-track` declares `--pano-unit` / `--pano-gap` / `--pano-pad` *on the element itself*, so setting those variables on an ancestor loses to the track's own declaration (inheritance always loses to a direct declaration). Today the only ways to change the rhythm are editing `Panorama.vue` or writing `:deep(.pano-track) { --pano-unit: … }`. For plug-and-play this wants either a `unit`/`gap`/`pad` prop bound inline (inline custom properties do win) or the defaults moved behind `var(--pano-unit, 50vw)`-style fallbacks. Unfixed.

**Edge 2 — the track must be given a height**
`.pano-inner` is `height: 100%` and sections are `height: 100%`, so the track needs a definite height from the consumer (`flex-1 min-h-0` in this repo). In a new project that's the one mandatory integration detail, and it's only in the doc, not enforced or warned about. Auto-height tracks will collapse to nothing.

**Smaller things**
- The 640px breakpoint is hardcoded twice (CSS media query + `matchMedia` in `PanoramaSection`); they agree, but changing it means editing both.
- Content taller than the section only scrolls if you pass `:scrollable="true"` — no warning otherwise.
- `:deep(.pano-inner)` vertical padding is consumer styling leaking through a deep selector; harmless, but it's the same escape hatch as Edge 1.

**And the repo still has two scrollers**
The project **detail** view hasn't been migrated, so the "one framework everywhere" claim only covers the main panorama. Until it's ported, this repo is plug-and-play for new sections but not yet a single-engine codebase.

Want me to fix Edge 1 (metric props with CSS-var fallbacks) and port the detail view? Those two would make it actually drop-in.

> Alternatively support both: props set input vars inline; consumers can also set input vars on track via `:style` fallthrough (class/style fallthrough merges with our inline style — consumer style wins). So advanced usage works without props. Good.

- path: src\components\panorama\framework\Panorama.vue
  content: |
      onMounted(() => {
          measure()
          window.addEventListener('resize', measure)
      
          if (track.value?.firstElementChild) {
              resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver(measure)
              resizeObserver.observe(track.value.firstElementChild)
          }
      })
      
      onUnmounted(() => {
          window.removeEventListener('resize', measure)
          resizeObserver?.disconnect()
          if (frame !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(frame)
      })
      
      defineExpose({ scrollToSection, scrollToIndex, activeIndex, activeSection, totalStops })
      </script>
      
      <template>
          <main
              ref="track"
              class="pano-track"
              :style="trackStyle"
              tabindex="0"
              @scroll.passive="syncScroll"
              @keydown="onKeydown"
          >
              <div class="pano-inner">
                  <slot />
                  <div class="pano-trailing" aria-hidden="true" />
              </div>
          </main>
      </template>
      
      <style scoped>
      /*
       * Panorama metrics. One regular section is --pano-unit wide, neighbors are
       * --pano-gap apart, and one tick travels --pano-stride. The *-base / *-sm
       * inputs come from props (or a consumer's inline style); the resolved tokens
       * are what sections read, so a prop override never beats the breakpoint.
       */
      .pano-track {
          --pano-unit: var(--pano-unit-base, 85vw);
          --pano-gap: var(--pano-gap-base, 1rem);
          --pano-pad: var(--pano-pad-base, 1.5rem);
          --pano-stride: calc(var(--pano-unit) + var(--pano-gap));
      
          position: relative;
          overflow-x: auto;
          overflow-y: hidden;
          scroll-snap-type: x mandatory;
          scroll-padding-left: var(--pano-pad);
          scroll-padding-right: var(--pano-pad);
          overscroll-behavior-x: contain;
          overscroll-behavior-y: contain;
          -webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch;
          outline: none;
      }
      
      @media (min-width: 640px) {
          .pano-track {
              --pano-unit: var(--pano-unit-sm, 50vw);
              --pano-gap: var(--pano-gap-sm, 4rem);
              --pano-pad: var(--pano-pad-sm, 8rem);
          }
      }
      
      .pano-inner {
          display: flex;
          align-items: stretch;
          width: max-content;
          height: 100%;
          padding-left: var(--pano-pad);
      }
      
      /*
       * The last stop sits at pad + (totalStops - 1) * stride, so the track needs at
       * least its own width minus one stride of slack past the final section to reach
       * it without the scroll position being clamped short.

Both done.

**Edge 1 — rhythm is now a prop API**
`Panorama` takes `unit` / `gap` / `pad` (apply at every breakpoint) and `unitSm` / `gapSm` / `padSm` (override from 640px). The CSS defaults moved behind fallbacks, so passing nothing reproduces the old behaviour exactly:

```css
--pano-unit: var(--pano-unit-base, 85vw);
@media (min-width: 640px) { --pano-unit: var(--pano-unit-sm, var(--pano-unit-base, 50vw)); }
```

Two bugs found while wiring it: an override for one breakpoint originally leaked nowhere useful (`gap="0rem"` still hit the desktop `4rem` fallback), and the trailing slack was hardcoded to `100vw`, which is wrong for a track that isn't viewport-width. The track now measures itself into `--pano-viewport` and the spacer uses `trackWidth − stride`.

**Detail view ported**
`PanoramaProjectDetail.vue` is now `<Panorama unit="88vw" unit-sm="65vw" gap="0rem" pad="0rem">` with one `PanoramaSection` per panel (`overview`, `details`, `shot-N`, all `scrollable`). The hand-written `.panorama-scroll` / `.panorama-panel` rules and the `w-[12vw] md:w-[35vw]` trailing div are gone — that slack now falls out of the metrics (100vw − 88vw mobile, 100vw − 65vw desktop, i.e. exactly the old 12vw/35vw). Net effect: the detail view snaps one panel per gesture for the first time, and its ArrowLeft/Right keys work via the framework. Screenshot panels keep their look through an inner `flex flex-1 items-center justify-center` wrapper, since the section body is the flex container now.

The panel height stayed `calc(100vh - 64px)`, and I verified Tailwind emitted it (`height:calc(100vh - 64px)` in the built CSS) — the first spelling `h-[calc(100vh-64px)]` was invalid (calc needs spaces; `_` escapes them).

**Verification**: `npm run build` passes after every change; ESLint reports only the two pre-existing `v-html` warnings. **Not verified**: real gesture snapping and the visual result — still no browser here, so `npm run dev` and a swipe through both views is the outstanding check (especially: wide projects section at ~2× unit, detail panels butting together with no gaps, and the last stop of each track aligning to the padding).

      <script setup lang="ts">
      import { computed, nextTick, onMounted, onUnmounted, provide, ref, watch } from 'vue'
      import { PANORAMA_CONTEXT, type PanoramaRegistration } from './panorama-context'
      
      /*
       * Optional metric overrides. Values are any CSS length; when omitted the CSS
       * fallbacks apply (mobile 85vw/1rem/1.5rem, desktop 50vw/4rem/8rem).
       */
      const props = defineProps<{
          unit?: string
          gap?: string
          pad?: string
          unitSm?: string
          gapSm?: string
          padSm?: string
      }>()
      
      const emit = defineEmits<{
          'section-change': [id: string]
          'index-change': [index: number]
      }>()
      
      const track = ref<HTMLElement | null>(null)
      const registrations = ref<PanoramaRegistration[]>([])
      const scrollLeft = ref(0)
      const metrics = ref({ stride: 0, pad: 0 })
      const trackWidth = ref(0)
      
      const trackStyle = computed(() => ({
          '--pano-unit-base': props.unit,
          '--pano-gap-base': props.gap,
          '--pano-pad-base': props.pad,
          '--pano-unit-sm': props.unitSm,
          '--pano-gap-sm': props.gapSm,
          '--pano-pad-sm': props.padSm,
          '--pano-viewport': trackWidth.value > 0 ? `${trackWidth.value}px` : undefined,
      }))
      
      let frame: number | null = null
      let resizeObserver: ResizeObserver | null = null
      
      const clamp = (value: number, min: number, max: number) => Math.min(Math.max(value, min), max)
      
      /* Sections own their span; the framework only accumulates it into stops. */
      const register = (entry: PanoramaRegistration) => {
          if (!registrations.value.some((item) => item.id === entry.id)) {
              registrations.value.push(entry)
          }
      }
      
      const unregister = (id: string) => {
          registrations.value = registrations.value.filter((item) => item.id !== id)
      }
      
      provide(PANORAMA_CONTEXT, { register, unregister })
      
      const stops = computed(() => {
          let cursor = 0
          return registrations.value.map((entry) => {
              const span = Math.max(1, Math.round(entry.getSpan()))
              const stop = { id: entry.id, span, start: cursor }
              cursor += span
              return stop
          })
      })
      
      const totalStops = computed(() => stops.value.reduce((total, stop) => total + stop.span, 0))
      
      /* One measure pass reads the standard metrics off the CSS, never content width. */
      const measure = () => {
          const el = track.value
          if (!el) return
      
          trackWidth.value = el.clientWidth
      
          const pad = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(el).scrollPaddingLeft) || 0
          const first = el.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[data-pano-section]')
      
          if (!first) {
              metrics.value = { stride: 0, pad }
              return
          }
      
          const gap = Number.parseFloat(getComputedStyle(first).marginRight) || 0
          const span = stops.value[0]?.span ?? 1
          const unit = (first.getBoundingClientRect().width - (span - 1) * gap) / span
      
          metrics.value = { stride: unit + gap, pad }
      }
      
      const syncScroll = () => {
          if (frame !== null) return
          frame = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
              frame = null
              scrollLeft.value = track.value?.scrollLeft ?? 0
          })
      }
      
      const prefersReducedMotion = () =>
          typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches
      
      const scrollToIndex = (index: number) => {
          const el = track.value
          const { stride, pad } = metrics.value
          if (!el || stride <= 0) return
      
          el.scrollTo({
              left: clamp(index, 0, Math.max(0, totalStops.value - 1)) * stride + pad,
              behavior: prefersReducedMotion() ? 'auto' : 'smooth',
          })
      }
      
      const scrollToSection = (id: string, options: { lead?: number } = {}) => {
          const stop = stops.value.find((item) => item.id === id)
          if (!stop) return
          scrollToIndex(stop.start - (options.lead ?? 0))
      }
      
      const activeIndex = computed(() => {
          const { stride, pad } = metrics.value
          if (stride <= 0 || totalStops.value === 0) return 0
          return clamp(Math.round((scrollLeft.value - pad) / stride), 0, totalStops.value - 1)
      })
      
      const activeSection = computed(
          () =>
              stops.value.find(
                  (stop) => activeIndex.value >= stop.start && activeIndex.value < stop.start + stop.span,
              )?.id ?? '',
      )
      
      const onKeydown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
          if (event.key === 'ArrowRight') {
              event.preventDefault()
              scrollToIndex(activeIndex.value + 1)
          } else if (event.key === 'ArrowLeft') {
              event.preventDefault()
              scrollToIndex(activeIndex.value - 1)
          }
      }
      
      watch(activeSection, (id) => {
          if (id) emit('section-change', id)
      })
      
      watch(activeIndex, (index) => emit('index-change', index))
      
      watch(totalStops, () => nextTick(measure))
      
      onMounted(() => {
          measure()
          window.addEventListener('resize', measure)
      
          if (track.value?.firstElementChild) {
              resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver(measure)
              resizeObserver.observe(track.value.firstElementChild)
          }
      })
      
      onUnmounted(() => {
          window.removeEventListener('resize', measure)
          resizeObserver?.disconnect()
          if (frame !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(frame)
      })
